Ajman Government Media Office unveils vision and programme for inaugural Ajman Media Forum 2026

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

An interactive programme featuring media innovation challenges, emerging technologies and data-driven dialogue

AJMAN, UAE, Nov. 6, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- The Ajman Government Media Office unveiled the strategic vision, objectives, and key themes of the inaugural Ajman Media Forum 2026 â THE SIGNAL, during a press conference at Thara Centre for Entrepreneurship in Ajman. Scheduled to take place on 18 and 19 November under the theme 'Who Owns the Signal?', the Forum will bring together strategic partners and feature a programme combining thought-provoking dialogue, practical experiences and technological innovation.

The Forum forms part of Ajman's efforts to strengthen its position as a regional hub for media dialogue and innovation, bringing together local and international expertise across the media, technology, creative economy and scientific research sectors. It aims to explore the transformations reshaping the industry while advancing trust, credibility and innovation in an increasingly dynamic media landscape.

HE Mohammed Abdullah Al Kaabi, Executive Director of the Ajman Government Media Office, said: "The launch of the Forum comes amid profound transformations across the media landscape, as the ability to shape narratives and influence public opinion increasingly extends beyond traditional media institutions and channels. Digital platforms, algorithms, artificial intelligence and content creators are playing a growing role in determining how information is produced, distributed and consumed."

He added: "Against this backdrop, the Forum takes 'Who Owns the Signal?' as its central question, opening a multidisciplinary dialogue on the changing dynamics of influence, the future of trust and credibility, and the responsibility of media and government institutions in shaping a more innovative and reliable media environment."

HE Al Kaabi explained that the Forum's programme is structured around five key themes: media transformations and the future of the industry; artificial intelligence in content creation; building trust and countering disinformation; national reputation and government communication; and digital platforms and the media economy. Together, these themes reflect an integrated approach to media that brings together technology, economics, communication, culture and social responsibility.

He affirmed that the Forum is supported by a network of strategic partners, including the National Media Authority, TRENDS Group, the Department of Economic Development â Ajman, Abu Dhabi Media Network, Arabian Radio Network (ARN), the Municipality and Planning Department â Ajman, and the Transport Authority in Ajman. Each partner will contribute its expertise and capabilities to enriching the programme and expanding its impact.

The partnership with the National Media Authority will support the development of a platform that brings together expertise, empowers national talent and creates new opportunities for collaboration and innovation.

As part of efforts to advance the creative economy, the Department of Economic Development â Ajman will participate as the Creative Economy Development Partner, with a dedicated pavilion showcasing UAE-based creative media companies. The pavilion will enable participating companies to present their work and solutions, build professional connections, and explore opportunities for collaboration and investment.

TRENDS Group will participate through a dedicated platform highlighting the capabilities of its five companies across scientific research, strategic consultancy, training, artificial intelligence and future foresight. The Group will also contribute to a keynote session, offering research-driven perspectives on the transformations reshaping the media sector. Its participation reflects the importance of collaboration between media institutions, think tanks and research centres in advancing knowledge and supporting evidence-based decision-making.

The Municipality and Planning Department â Ajman will participate as the Quality of Life and Urban Sustainability Sponsor, highlighting media's role in showcasing development projects and raising community awareness. The Department will present its AM30x30 agenda and related initiatives and projects to support Ajman's urban development and enhance quality of life.

Meanwhile, the Transport Authority in Ajman, as the Official Transport Partner, will facilitate transportation for VIPs and Forum guests, support the promotional campaign across its transport channels, and host a dedicated pavilion showcasing its services and initiatives.

Abu Dhabi Media Network will help expand the forum's reach and bring its discussions and insights to wider audiences through its television, print and digital platforms. Meanwhile, Arabian Radio Network (ARN) will strengthen the forum's presence across radio and audio platforms, engaging listeners and extending the conversation to new audiences.

Beyond its panel discussions and presentations, the forum will offer a series of interactive experiences, including the Mediathon, which will bring participants together to tackle real-world media challenges and develop practical ideas, projects and solutions through innovation, collaboration and hands-on learning.

The forum will also host a roundtable bringing together media leaders and decision-makers to examine the major shifts reshaping the industry. Drawing on data, research and global indicators, and supported by specialised knowledge partners, including Statista, the discussions will seek to generate actionable recommendations that address emerging challenges, anticipate future developments and help shape the sector's priorities.

The 'AI Media Lab' will offer visitors a hands-on opportunity to explore the technologies transforming the media landscape. Through live demonstrations of AI-powered content generation, news verification and other emerging applications, participants will be able to test these tools, understand their capabilities and explore their potential uses across the industry.

Complementing these experiences, 'Ajman Studios' will provide dedicated spaces for discussions and interviews with media leaders, industry experts and influential content creators. The studios will also support the production of original content, capturing key insights and perspectives from the forum and sharing them with audiences across multiple platforms, extending the impact of its discussions beyond the two-day event.

The forum will further expand its international dimension by spotlighting China's advances in media and technology innovation. Specialised companies and institutions will showcase their expertise, technologies and practical applications in artificial intelligence, media and content creation, creating opportunities for knowledge exchange, collaboration and stronger ties between the UAE and China.

By bringing together strategic partnerships, industry dialogue and hands-on experiences, Ajman Media Forum 2026 aims to create a platform where ideas translate into practical outcomes. Through this approach, the forum seeks to advance understanding of the forces shaping the media industry, encourage the development of innovative solutions and reinforce Ajman's position as a centre for regional and international dialogue on the future of media.

Hyperliquid Strategies seeks futures registration for perps

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The HYPE treasury company spent $785 million on tokens during the quarter and wants to bring Hyperliquid-based perpetual futures to the US.

A subsidiary of Nasdaq-listed HYPE treasury company Hyperliquid Strategies has applied to become a futures commission merchant (FCM), seeking to bring Hyperliquid-based perpetual futures to the US.

“Owning HYPE is how we aim to capture economics associated with the Hyperliquid protocol; setting up an FCM is how we intend to help bring markets deployed on Hyperliquid onshore,” said Hyperliquid Strategies CEO David Schamis in the company’s results for the quarter ended Sept. 30, published on Thursday.

Hyperliquid’s HIP-3 framework allows independent operators to launch custom perpetual futures markets on the protocol’s onchain infrastructure by staking 500,000 HYPE tokens ($38.8 million). Proposed HIP-3 features would allow operators to create permissioned markets that restrict participation to approved addresses.

The subsidiary would handle US customer accounts for permissioned markets deployed on Hyperliquid, the company said. Both the FCM application and the proposed markets require regulatory approval.

Intercontinental Exchange, the parent company of the New York Stock Exchange, announced an agreement with Trade[XYZ] on Oct. 29 to bring Hyperliquid-based perpetual markets to the US, subject to regulatory approvals. ICE plans to operate a dedicated market and clearing structure.

Related: Hyperliquid, Pump.fun account for nearly 90% of record $638M crypto buybacks: FT

During the quarter, Hyperliquid Strategies raised $911 million in equity capital. It spent $785 million to acquire 9.1 million HYPE and $17 million to repurchase 2.7 million PURR shares. The company held a total of 38.5 million HYPE as of Sept. 30, valued at $3.5 billion at the time.

“Hyperliquid is winning the parts of onchain finance that generate real fees, and nearly all of that value flows back to HYPE holders through programmatic buybacks,” Schamis said.

Hyperliquid Strategies (PURR) stock, 1-year chart. Source: Yahoo Finance

Hyperliquid Strategies’ shares fell 9.6% Thursday, closing at $8.76, according to Yahoo Finance.

Broadcom Stock Has Had Only 2 Losing Years Since 2009. 2026 Is on Pace to Be the 3rd.

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

After the last two, the stock rose 24% and then almost doubled. Can it rebound like that again?

2026 hasn't been good to Broadcom (AVGO -10.43%) stock. At roughly $300 as I write this, the chip designer's shares trade about 13% under their 2025 close of $346.10.

A losing year is rare for this stock. Since its August 2009 listing as Avago Technologies, the stock has ended just two calendar years lower. Shares dipped around 1% in 2018 and dropped 16% in 2022. But both times, a strong year followed, with the stock gaining 24% in 2019 and almost doubling in 2023.

So it's tempting to read this year's drop as the setup for another rebound. I'm not sure the pattern holds this time, though. The S&P 500 (^GSPC -4.36%) dropped around 6% in 2018 and 19% in 2022, so Broadcom beat the index both times. This year, the index is up about 9%.

If 2026 ended today, it'd be the first calendar year since its debut that Broadcom lagged the S&P 500 by over 5 percentage points.

Neither earlier drop was about the business. Broadcom's revenue climbed 18% in fiscal 2018 to almost $21 billion. Free cash flow grew even faster, up 50%. Fiscal 2022 revenue rose 21% to a record $33.2 billion.

Strangely, the rebound years were the slower ones. In June 2019, Broadcom cut its fiscal 2019 revenue forecast to $22.5 billion from $24.5 billion, as CEO Hock Tan pointed to "a broad-based slowdown in the demand environment." Revenue finished the year up 8%. And the stock's 24% rise in 2019 lagged the S&P 500's 29%, so that rebound mainly rode the market.

Fiscal 2023 revenue also rose just 8%. But Tan said that growth came from "investments in accelerators and network connectivity for AI by hyperscalers," and Broadcom forecast around $50 billion in fiscal 2024 revenue with VMware included, up 40%.

The starting price probably helped in both cases. Shares closed 2018 at around 12 times earnings and 2022 at about 15 times earnings, using non-GAAP (adjusted) earnings per share for the fiscal year just ended.

Broadcom's business is growing faster today than in any of those years. Revenue jumped 86% year over year to $29.6 billion in the fiscal third quarter of 2026 (the period ending Aug. 2, 2026), led by artificial intelligence (AI) semiconductor revenue of $16.7 billion, up 221%. Net income climbed 216%. And free cash flow has grown for three straight quarters, from $7.5 billion in fiscal Q4 2025 to $13.7 billion in fiscal Q3.

But shares have shed more than a third of their value since closing at a record $481.57 on June 2.

The stock also doesn't start at a cheap price. Shares trade at around 30 times adjusted earnings for the past four reported quarters, at least twice either earlier starting point. On fiscal 2027's expected earnings, though, the price-to-earnings ratio is about 16, which arguably looks fair for a business growing so fast.

Broadcom has said it plans to release its fiscal fourth-quarter results on Wednesday, Dec. 9.

Management guided for fiscal fourth-quarter revenue of about $34.8 billion, up 93% year over year, with $21.7 billion in AI semiconductor revenue.

The bigger test is fiscal 2027, when Broadcom expects AI semiconductor revenue to double to around $115 billion.

"This AI revenue guidance is being provided to you to give you the trajectory of our growth, and we do not intend to update it on a quarterly basis," CFO Amie Thuener said on the Sept. 2 earnings call.

So December's guidance for the fiscal first quarter of 2027 might be the closest check investors get. Hitting $115 billion takes an average of almost $29 billion in AI revenue a quarter, well over the $21.7 billion guided for the fiscal fourth quarter. The December report has also usually brought the annual dividend increase, which was 10% last year.

When I wrote in late September that Broadcom had climbed in 13 straight fourth quarters, shares were around $350. A gain of about 17% from here would continue that streak, and about 15% would keep 2026 out of the loss column.

Ultimately, Broadcom's business looks far stronger now than it did in 2018 or 2022. But this year's drop is Broadcom's own, not the market's, and shares don't start out cheap. Sure, a soft first-quarter outlook may send the stock down before the year ends.

I still think the stock is worth a look at this price. I'd just want to see that outlook before counting on another year like 2023.

Daniel Sparks and his clients do not have positions in any of the stocks mentioned. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends Broadcom. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

Uragano Como sul Lens, il ds: "Nessuno si dimette, ma la viviamo male: non siamo da Champions"

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

In Italia ci stiamo concentrando sulla bellezza del Como di Cesc Fabregas, che nella sua prima volta in Champions League e dopo appena tre partite ha già fatto aggiornare diversi record della competizione. Dall'altro lato però c'è una squadra, il Lens, che ha a che fare con i segni della devastazione derivanti dall'umiliante 9-1 incassato l'altra sera.

Il direttore sportivo del Lens, Jean-Louis Leca, ha rilasciato un'intervista a L'Equipe nella quale non ha usato mezzi termini per descrivere la situazione, confermando però che né lui, né il tecnico Cahuzac stanno pensando alle dimissioni.

Queste le sue parole: "Non mentiamo a noi stessi: non siamo al livello della Champions League. È così ma non significa che questo 9-1 sia normale. Non è normale. La Champions League tende creare tensione nell'ambiente. È un po' come se rompesse il nostro flusso positivo in campionato", spiega il dirigente.

Questa pesante sconfitta pesa anche sul bilancio di Yannick Cahuzak, nominato alla guida di Lens dopo l'espulsione di Dino Toppmöller, ma il suo futuro non è messo in discussione, assicura Jean-Louis Leca. "Nessuno pensa di andarsene. Né lui, né noi", ha deciso il direttore sportivo. "Se ci avesse proposto le sue dimissioni? Le avremmo rifiutate. Bisogna confrontare ciò che è paragonabile: durante la stagione di Champions League del 2023-2024, avevamo comprato per 66 milioni di euro e avevamo un budget salariale superiore di 15 milioni di euro rispetto a quella della stagione in corso. Dopo, capisco che le persone si interrogano su questo 9-1. Lo viviamo male, anche noi. Fa parte del lavoro prendere colpi come questo".

CytomX (CTMX) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Lower diarrhea rates emerge, but toxicity control remains the key test.

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

CytomX Therapeutics, Inc. (CTMX -8.94%) reported third quarter financial results and outlined a strategic transition toward late-stage clinical development for its lead program, Varseta-M. Management emphasized the first-in-class status of the EpCAM-directed antibody-drug conjugate, noting that its proprietary PROBODY platform enables the targeting of antigens previously considered undruggable. The company is advancing Varseta-M toward its first potential registrational study in metastatic colorectal cancer during the first half of 2027, while simultaneously initiating studies in earlier-line combination therapies and other gastrointestinal malignancies. Furthermore, the company maintains a cash runway through late 2028, supported by its collaboration with Regeneron and disciplined capital allocation toward its most compelling clinical priorities.

Operator: Good afternoon, everyone. Thank you for standing by. Welcome to the CytomX Therapeutics third quarter 26 financial results call. Please be advised that today's call is being recorded. I would now like to hand the call over to your host for today, Christopher W. Ogden, CytomX's chief financial officer. Please go ahead.

Christopher W. Ogden: Thank you. Good afternoon, and thank you for joining us. Before we begin, I would like to remind everyone that during this call, we will be making forward-looking statements. Because forward-looking statements relate to the future, they are subject to inherent uncertainties and risks that are difficult to predict and many of which are outside of our control. Important risks and uncertainties are set forth in our most recent public filings with the SEC, at sec.gov. Except as required by law, we undertake no obligation to update any forward-looking whether as a result of new information, future developments or otherwise.

Earlier this afternoon, we filed our quarterly report on Form 10 Q for Q3 26 and issued a press release with a summary of our third quarter 26 financial results and business highlights. We encourage everyone to read today's filing and press release. Additionally, the press release recording of this call and our SEC filings can be found under the Investors and News section of our website. With me on the call today is Dr. Sean McCarthy, CytomX Chief Executive Officer and Chairman. Sean will provide an update on our pipeline and company progress, before I cover the financials for the quarter. We will then conclude with a Q&A session. With that, I will turn the call over to Sean.

Sean A. McCarthy DPHIL: Thanks, Christopher, and good afternoon, everyone. We are very pleased to be here today to provide an update on our third quarter developments and what continues to be a highly productive year for CytomX as we advance our lead program, Varseta-M, towards its first potential registrational study in the first half of 27. Varseta-M is an EpCAM targeting topo-1 antibody-drug conjugate with strong potential in colorectal cancer and potentially other solid tumors. Importantly, to our knowledge, Varseta-M is first in class as the only EpCAM directed ADC in clinical development. Viceta-M has been intentionally designed and uniquely enabled by our PROBODY platform, creating a significant opportunity for differentiation and long term value creation.

Strategically, we are initially developing Varseta-M in metastatic colorectal cancer as the lead indication. Our sense of urgency for Varseta-M is driven by the significant unmet need in colorectal cancer which is the second leading cause of cancer death in the United States and worldwide. Varseta-M is on the leading edge of ADC innovation in CRC and has the potential to be 1 of the first monotherapy ADCs to market for the treatment of this very challenging cancer type. In terms of commercial potential, there are an estimated 35 thousand addressable patients in the United States with third-line or later CRC.

Given the anticancer activity we have reported previously, across an unselected late stage patient population and the limited clinical benefit of existing therapies for these patients, we see considerable opportunity for Varseta-M. Based on Varseta-M's unique profile, we are actively advancing our development program across 3 strategic areas of priority. First, we aim to move Varseta-M monotherapy into a registrational study in the first half of next year enabling a potential first approval and meaningful market opportunity in metastatic CRC. Second, we continue our development of Varseta-M in combinations, including with bevacizumab and with earlier line chemotherapy regimens with the goal of replacing current standard of care.

And third, we have started to evaluate Varseta-M's pan tumor potential across multiple EpCAM expressing cancers initially in additional gastrointestinal indications. The monotherapy for Varseta-M Phase 1 study has enrolled more than 100 patients across dose-escalation, expansion, and optimization cohorts. As previously reported, patients enrolled in the phase 1 study have been heavily pretreated with most patients having received 3 or more prior treatments in the metastatic setting. In March, we reported encouraging progression-free survival at doses of 8.6 and 10 milligrams per kilogram that exceeded current standard of care. We also shared our increasing understanding of safety profile of Varseta-M, particularly how to lower and manage rates of high grade diarrhea.

This initial experience with Varceta m in very late-line patients will provide us with a highly relevant dataset to enable monotherapy dose selection and inform the potential first registrational study design. Most recently, we have been highly focused on dose optimization, evaluating 2 doses, 8.6 and 10 milligrams per kilogram given every 3 weeks with adjusted ideal body weight dosing and upfront prophylaxis to manage gastrointestinal adverse events.

We anticipate providing an integrated update in January across the phase 1 study, including our potential registrational strategy, and the data that underpin our plans. it is important to note here that we expect this next update to provide the first look at overall survival for Varseta-M which we anticipate will be the primary endpoint in our initial registrational study. We look forward to providing this next update in the near term. Now moving on to the Varseta-M combination development program. Our ongoing phase 1 study evaluating Varseta-M in combination with bevacizumab continues to enroll late-line CRC patients to assess dose and safety. We view this combination as a foundational first step in evaluating Varseta-M's potential in earlier-line CRC.

Subject to data from the ongoing phase 1 study we anticipate moving to earlier line CRC patients in the future evaluation of this combination and we anticipate reporting initial clinical data from the bevacizumab combination in the first half of 27. To also enable the use of Varseta-M in earlier lines of CRC therapy, we remain on track to initiate a study evaluating Varseta-M in combination with bevacizumab, 5-fluorouracil and leucovorin. By the end of the year. This study will be a new Phase 1/2 trial that would enroll second line CRC patients once phase 1 dose finding is successfully completed.

We believe these Varceta m combination studies have the potential to inform future development options and look forward to providing initial updates starting next year. Now moving to the development of Varseta-M outside of CRC, our strategy is to develop and ultimately commercialize Varseta-M across multiple EpCAM expressing solid tumors. And we have recently initiated phase 1 cohorts in gastrointestinal indications with substantial unmet clinical need. and that we view as highly complementary to CRC from a commercial perspective. Have initiated cohorts in unselected gastric and gastroesophageal junction cancer, as well as EpCAM selected pancreatic ductal adenocarcinoma and EPCAM selected biliary tract cancer.

Each of these EpCAM expressing tumor types currently have limited treatment options in later-line settings, and may present expedited development pathways for Varseta-M upon clinical proof-of-concept. Our initial plan is to enroll approximately 20 patients per tumor type to evaluate Varseta-M's antitumor activity and safety before assessing later phase development options. Having focused for Varseta-M exclusively in CRC for the first 2 years of development, we are very excited to now be evaluating Varseta-M's broader clinical potential. Now turning to CX-801, our masked interferon alpha 2 b program, being evaluated in checkpoint refractory melanoma.

We see CX-801 as an exciting and broad to reprogram the immune responsiveness of solid tumors and we continue to enroll the ongoing phase 1 study evaluating CX-801 as a monotherapy and in combination with KEYTRUDA. Enrollment in the monotherapy cohort of our phase 1 study continues. Having exceeded dose levels of the approved dose of unmasked interferon alpha 2 b. Initial biomarker data for CX-801 monotherapy presented previously have reinforced our mechanism of action hypothesis for CX-801 in combination with KEYTRUDA. We continue to enroll in the combination cohort, and we look forward to sharing initial clinical data from the phase 1 study in melanoma in the first half of 27.

With those updates, let me now transition the call back to Christopher.

Christopher W. Ogden: Thank you, Sean. As Sean highlighted, we are making important progress across the Varseta-M program. Including potentially moving towards later stage development. As we progress the broadening Varseta-M development program, we have continued to maintain an efficient and flexible cost structure enabling us to pursue the opportunities Sean described earlier. To that end, with our current balance sheet, we project cash runway into the second half of 28. Importantly, our runway funds our key value creation priorities that Sean outlined. Including advancing Varseta-M into its first potential registrational study pursuing combination development to move Varseta-M into earlier lines of CRC, and then expanding into multiple other EpCAM expressing gastrointestinal tumors.

With that, I will now walk through our third quarter financial results. As of September 30, 2026, we ended the quarter with $348 million in cash, cash equivalents and investments. Versus $330 million in cash as of June 30, 2026. Looking at revenue and operating expenses for the quarter, total revenue was $1.7 million compared to $6 million in the third quarter of 25. The decrease in revenue was primarily attributed to the conclusion of the Astellas collaboration in the second quarter of 26.

Operating expenses for the third quarter were $25.2 million compared to $21.7 million in the third quarter of 25. Research and development expenses were $16.5 million during the third quarter, representing an increase of $1.2 million versus the third quarter of 25, primarily due to increased personnel related costs, and partially offset by lower Varseta-M spending due to the timing of manufacturing activities. General and administrative expenses increased by $2.3 million during the 3 ending September thirtieth 26, to $8.7 million compared to $6.4 million for the corresponding period in 2025. Increased G&A expenses were primarily driven by personnel related expenses and higher consulting spend.

I think it is a good question, but I would reframe it a little bit in terms of ultimately, what is the dose? Of course, what is the absolute dose that a patient receives and what is the dose intensity achieved over time. And that is the type of data that we are going to be analyzing and that we will be sharing in due course.

Olivia Brayer: Okay. Understood. Looking forward to it. Thank you very much.

Operator: Thank you. Moment for our next question. Our next question comes from the line of Kalpit Patel of Wolfe. Your line is now open.

Kalpit Patel: Hi. This is Nikki on for Kalpit. We wanted to ask if, as you gain additional experience with the optimized dosing regimen, you would be able to give us any granularity on what that selected dose might be for the phase 3 or what you are leaning towards? Thank you so much.

Sean A. McCarthy DPHIL: Yeah. Thanks for the question. cannot provide any additional color at this moment in time. But that will be an important component of the update in January. Thank you.

Operator: Thank you. I am not showing any further questions in the queue. I would now like to turn it back over to Dr. Sean McCarthy, Chairman and CEO, for closing remarks.

Sean A. McCarthy DPHIL: Thank you, and thanks, everyone, for listening in today. We continue to be highly encouraged by progress we are making here at CytomX with Varseta-M. With CX-801, and more broadly with our Probody therapeutic platform. We are excited about the breadth of opportunities in front of us as we drive toward the end of the year. and, rapidly as it seems, into 2027. So thank you all for your time today.

FIGS (FIGS) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Guidance rose as international sales neared a fifth of the business.

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

FIGS, Inc. (FIGS -7.89%) reported its fourth consecutive quarter of revenue growth exceeding 25%, leading management to raise full-year 2026 guidance for net revenue and margins. The company experienced an acceleration in active customer growth and average order value, while its international segment expanded to nearly 20% of the total business mix. Management indicated that while supply chain transitions from Jordan to Southeast Asia continue to impact inventory levels, core stock levels are improving in the fourth quarter. The company also expanded its non-scrubwear category through the acquisition of V Coterie and the launch of jewelry and accessories under the CHARxMACY brand.

Operator: We will now hand the conference over to Tom Shaw, Senior Vice President of Investor Relations. Please go ahead.

Tom Shaw: Good afternoon, and thank you for joining us to discuss FIGS' third quarter 2026 results, which we released this afternoon and can be found in our earnings press release and in the shareholder presentation posted to our investor relations website at ir.wearFIGS.com. Presenting on today's call are Trina Spear, our Co-Founder and Chief Executive Officer, and Sarah Oughtred, our Chief Financial Officer. As a reminder, remarks on this call that do not concern past events are forward-looking statements. These may include predictions, expectations, or estimates, including about future financial performance, market opportunity, or business plans. Forward-looking statements include risk and uncertainties, and actual results could differ materially.

These and other risks are discussed in our SEC filings, including in the 10-Q we filed today. Do not place undue reliance on forward-looking statements, which speak only as of today, and which we undertake no obligation to update. Finally, we will discuss certain non-GAAP metrics and key performance indicators, which we believe are useful supplemental measures for understanding our business. Definitions and reconciliations of these non-GAAP measures to their most comparable GAAP measures are included in our shareholder presentation. Now, I would like to turn the call over to Trina.

Trina Spear: Thanks, Tom. Good afternoon, everyone, and thank you for joining us. FIGS delivered another remarkable quarter, exceeding expectations on both the top and bottom line. Net revenues grew 26% to approximately $191 million, well ahead of our 20% outlook and marking our fourth straight quarter of greater than 25% growth. Strength was once again broad-based across categories, channels, and geographies. Our customer data was highlighted by an acceleration in year-over-year growth across three key measures. This included active customer growth of 14% to nearly 3.2 million, average order value growth of 11% to $126, and net revenues per active customer growth of 13% to another FIGS record of $236.

In addition to our top-line success, we also delivered increased profitability despite the elevated overall cost environment. Our adjusted EBITDA margin expanded to 16.5%, up 410 basis points year-over-year, surpassing our outlook of approximately 14%. Gross margin remained incredibly strong at over 69%, and we saw greater than planned SG&A leverage, which again highlights the power of our model as we scale. We continue to put capital to work as we repurchased approximately $14 million of our shares during the period, following the additional $100 million buyback authorization announced in August. We have now delivered four amazing quarters in a row.

Over this time, the consistent question we have received has been, how do we drive this sharp inflection in the business? The simple answer is that it has been through the combined efforts of great products, marketing campaigns that resonate deeply, a community that we are incredibly connected with, and strong industry fundamentals. But that simplicity understates the reality. Executing on all of this was not easy. It required a step change across a range of areas where we had always been successful, but where we knew we had to get even better.

At the heart of this work is the amazing team we have assembled over the past several years, with strong leaders and support fortifying our efforts across product, marketing, supply chain, finance, international teams, and Community Hubs. Their contributions and energy have been pivotal in unlocking this powerful story that has been there all along. On the product side, we have always had category-defining core offerings. What has changed is the sophistication we have added to every part of our go-to-market approach, which really began to take hold in the second half of 2025.

We are now more aligned than ever in how we understand and dictate style, deliver impactful color, improve fit, diversify our fabrics, enhance our merchandising, and invest in inventory across both core and limited edition. On the brand side, we renewed our focus on the top of the funnel, anchored by last year's "Where Do You Wear FIGS?" campaign and extending this year with "Never Change." We leveraged key viral moments from the Olympics to Noah Lyles at the Emmys, magnified our community support through expanded advocacy, and built greater sophistication across the full funnel.

This collective impact is real and differentiated, and we have powered these efforts in product and brand to serve an industry that has become increasingly rare in today's landscape. In addition to the replenishment-driven and essential nature of healthcare apparel, the job backdrop is strong, with healthcare-related jobs powering the economy. This is predicted to remain true going far into the future. Indeed, even amid fears of AI disruption, the Bureau of Labor Statistics projects the sector will add the most jobs over the next decade, about 37% of all new jobs. This is a fundamentally human profession, and we are proud to have built an authentic community around it.

Just as we know this industry is incredibly strong, being successful depends on creating strong human connections to the healthcare professionals who power it. We have seen a number of longstanding players in the industry fall on hard times at the same time we have accelerated, and we believe it is our ability to drive product innovation, impact, and community connection that puts us on a different path. Even with this run of strong performance, we are focused on what is next, sustaining our momentum and driving growth over the long run. Importantly, we see our recent efforts and success as foundational.

They were never designed to simply be one-time wins, but rather sustainable building blocks that drive enduring impact for healthcare professionals. Great foundations enable further progress, and we are setting our sights across a multitude of opportunities to raise the high bar we have set again. For instance, in product, our enhanced design capabilities are just now starting to have a meaningful impact. Our inventory positioning and depth is set to improve and be optimized, and we are accelerating our head-to-toe category expansion, thereby creating TAM. As we think about our brand's reach, we are far from the finish line as we look to serve more of the 100 million plus global healthcare community.

Domestically, we still have relatively low brand awareness, a digital experience that can become more personalized and engaging, and omnichannel connectivity that is untapped. Internationally, we are still under 20% of our total mix, with a number of key growth markets still in early stages. Physical in-person access to our brand remains highly limited, with just nine Community Hubs open by the end of the year, and the TEAMS B2B opportunity to unlock institutional healthcare needs remains ahead of us. Additionally, on impact, our efforts to serve those who serve others are constant.

This is a true brand differentiator and something our community deserves, and we are laser-focused on how we can scale this impact across our growing channels and geographies. Combining our momentum with these opportunities, we are optimistic about where we are going and how we can extend this unique growth story. Before diving deeper into product, brand, and market expansion, I want to update you on our supply chain following the Jordan developments we updated you on last quarter. While the WRO is still in effect, our own efforts are progressing. Our team and I recently traveled to Southeast Asia, both to support our existing Vietnamese supplier relationships and to further our diversification efforts.

We have secured additional capacity in Vietnam. Production is ramping, goods are being expedited, and inventory is improving real-time. In Q4, we are leveraging our existing partners to start our first production lines at facilities in Indonesia, which we expect will support a more meaningful mix of our business by the end of 2027. We are happy with our near-term progress, even as overall strength of demand extends some of our inventory replenishment efforts into Q1. Overall, this has been a complete team effort, particularly considering where our supplier base was positioned just 12 months ago. Now, let's turn to some of our product stories and where we are driving impact.

Our ongoing inflection in the business continues to showcase the growing sophistication in how we bring product and head-to-toe solutions to market. At the same time, our team is demonstrating the resilience needed to make impactful near-term changes to our calendar to mitigate supplier disruption. We delivered a range of impactful color collections throughout Q3, driving particular excitement on launch days. Success was broad-based and included our Spider-Man collaboration, back-to-school, a limited espresso drop, breast cancer awareness, and more. Our new durable FIBREx fabrication exceeded expectations, with highly positive social sentiment and customers specifically calling out our streetwear styling, our durable structured silhouette, and our overall design.

Speaking of design, we continue to differentiate our scrub pant offering with new waistband solutions across several of our best-selling styles. With our recent acquisition of V Coterie, we broadened our non-scrubwear opportunity with a dedicated collection of jewelry, pins, charms, and accessories designed specifically for healthcare professionals. This product officially debuted in August under what we are calling the CHARxMACY, creating a natural extension of the uniform by combining emotional engagement, personalization, and collectability. As we previously indicated, much of the original launch sold out, and we amassed a waiting list of nearly 10,000 healthcare professionals as we work to rebuild the assortment ahead of the holidays.

For Q4, we are off to a fast start with our fall drop, featuring a full return of our most requested color, espresso. The launch also showcased our unparalleled innovation flywheel, where we brought new fabric and style as part of our boucle outerwear assortment. Over the last few weeks, we have also started to get healthier from an inventory perspective, including in our core Rafaela and Kade styles, a process that will continue in the months ahead as we build supply and chase strong demand. We are excited to bring more newness across our FIBREx and FORMx fabrications, as well as our popular bag lines. The reach of our brand has never been broader or more impactful.

Impact trips are always a hallmark of our efforts to change healthcare. Through these trips, we travel the world with our FIGS ambassadors to extend support across a diverse range of healthcare needs. In July, we partnered with Operation Walk Los Angeles and St. Elizabeth Hospital to send a team to the Philippines to provide over 40 free joint replacements. We just recently continued our work in Kenya at the FIGS operating theater, the state-of-the-art hospital center we built in 2024. To date, this incredible facility has supported more than 2,500 surgeries that otherwise would not have been possible for this community of 500,000 Kenyans.

From advocacy to education, last month, we launched a dedicated FIGS moment with OOG Health, the AI-powered multidisciplinary education platform for healthcare professionals that is focused on redefining a broken continuing education industry and process. We made an investment in OOG two years ago, as we saw this opportunity align with our overall mission and significantly expand the ways that we can serve healthcare professionals. We have also now entered into a collaboration agreement with OOG that provides FIGS with a range of benefits, including on marketing, data, and AI.

On top of all these uniquely FIGS efforts, we have our big brand moments, and we are going big this week with the final chapter of our year-long Never Change campaign. This powerful bookend to our 2026 effort is designed to show all the good that lives within healthcare, and it is another example of what will never change across the community we are so proud to serve. Finally, I would be remiss not to mention our brand partner and friend, Noah Lyles, who People magazine just named as the Sexiest Man Alive for 2026.

To recognize our community, we partnered with People to show that sexy people save lives, putting a fun spotlight on six of our FIGS ambassadors as part of the overall feature. Turning to market expansion, starting with international. Third quarter net revenue growth surged 51% as our international mix approaches 20% of our total business. Our broad-based strength was particularly notable across Latin America, Mexico, the Middle East, and Europe, where we are seeing the benefits from our increased localization and community-building efforts. Overall, we are excited to see both strong traffic gains and incredible growth in our baseline days internationally, which we believe is another healthy sign of sustainable demand.

In TEAMS, we are showcasing our strategy to build a pipeline of larger opportunities. As examples, we are rolling out our recent partnerships with Bupa Dental Care, with nearly 400 dental centers in the U.K., and European Wax Center, with just over 1,000 aesthetic locations across the U.S. We believe these partnerships serve as blueprints for how to serve larger businesses in higher education, particularly as we continue to build solutions that simplify order complexity for a broader range of institutions. On the Community Hub side, we saw another quarter of double-digit comp store growth and non-comp performance exceeding our expectations.

All four of our 2026 openings are slated later this year: Tyson's Corner in the Washington, D.C. area, Fashion Square in Scottsdale, Aventura Mall in Miami, and Valley Fair near San Jose. We continue to progress with our real estate pipeline and expect a larger, more balanced store opening cadence in 2027. Importantly, the proof points remain compelling: a higher mix of new customers, a payback well under our two-year target, and accretive margins. In summary, our ongoing outperformance puts us on pace for our fastest full-year growth since our hyper-growth performance of 2021, when we were just about half the size we are today.

It has been a landmark year for FIGS, and I'm extremely proud of our ongoing execution and improving performance, even amid the noise of the macro, global uncertainty, and trade disruptions. As we focus on stepping into the new future, we are set up to grow Q4 in the mid-teens range on top of our blockbuster 33% growth in Q4 last year, accelerating our two-year stacked performance to nearly 50%. That is a powerful statement to exit the year. Along with top line, we are firmly focused on driving our adjusted EBITDA margin even higher. While we will not sacrifice investments to support the long-term aspirations of our brand, we do expect our discipline around profitability to continue in 2027.

Healthcare professionals are at the center of all we do. The needs they serve will not change, and neither will our commitment to showing up for them. With that, I will turn it over to Sarah to walk through our financial results and outlook.

Sarah Oughtred: Thanks, Trina. FIGS' strong Q3 continued an incredible four-quarter run of elevated growth, stronger profitability, and a bolstered balance sheet. I will start off with the details of our broad-based Q3 performance and then walk through our growing conviction in carrying this momentum into Q4 with our raised full-year outlook. Looking at the details of Q3, net revenues increased 26% year-over-year to $190.7 million, outpacing our outlook, calling for growth of approximately 20%. The breadth of our strength continued to stand out across scrubwear and non-scrubwear, domestically and abroad, and spanning our sales channels. Color launches were particularly impactful during the period, complemented by the ongoing success of our business-as-usual selling days without specific brand or product activations.

As anticipated, the back half of the quarter experienced some stockouts related to our Jordan supplier transition. Active customer growth accelerated for the fourth straight quarter, increasing 14% year-over-year to approach 3.2 million, and supported by a healthy balance of new and returning customers. Average order value increased 11% to $126, with balanced contributions from higher average unit retail and improved quality of sales. AUR gains were driven by both our early 2026 pricing action and product mix, while quality of sales was supported by lower discount and return rates. We were also encouraged by the continued trend in the reduction of days between purchases from our returning customers.

Together, these factors drove our trailing 12-month measure for net revenues per active customer up 13% to another FIGS record, $236. By category, scrubwear grew 24%, representing 83% of net revenues for the period. Core styles remained strong, powering demand throughout the quarter, while the breadth and impact of limited edition colors and expanded FIBREx styles drove robust sell-through and excitement. Non-scrubwear gained 35%, representing 17% of net revenues. The theme of broad-based growth was echoed here across categories. Underscrubs were supported by momentum in our core franchises and demand for new limited edition offerings. Outerwear was led by core scrub jackets and fleece, supporting our category optimism ahead of expansion efforts here in Q4.

Relative to historical cohorts, new customers are coming in with higher AOVs, above and beyond the impact of pricing, and sustaining this relative strength as they come back to the brand. Across returning customer cohorts, order frequency and spend are also improving. We believe these signs of increasing customer quality across our active customers are powerful building blocks for future growth. International growth continues to reflect the strong balance between new and returning customers. Trina highlighted the pronounced growth in Latin America, Mexico, the Middle East, and Europe, but we were also pleased with strong growth across the rest of the world.

While we have added 27 markets this year to reach 85 in total, we still generated nearly 90% of total international growth in Q3 from our existing comp markets. Gross margin for Q3 contracted 60 basis points to 69.3%, though ahead of our expectations and remaining quite strong. As a reminder, last year's comparable period was favorably impacted by our work to improve returns processing, which contributed to outsized performance for the period. While higher inventory reserves were also a headwind, we continued to see the positive impacts from pricing and efficiency efforts, which included costing, higher full price selling, and lower return rates.

Our selling expense for Q3 was $44.4 million, representing 23.3% of net revenues compared to 23.6% last year. The lower expense rate was primarily driven by net revenue leverage. Our successful ongoing work around outbound carrier optimization and fulfillment efficiencies has also helped offset ongoing fuel pressures, our supplier transitions, and international mix impact. Marketing expense for Q3 was $25.5 million, representing 13.4% of net revenues, down from 15.5% last year. Our Q3 performance reflected the impacts of net revenue leverage as well as digital marketing efficiencies. These gains continue to fuel great support in driving international brand awareness and other brand-enhancing partnerships. G&A for Q3 was $40 million, representing 21% of net revenues compared to 24.5% last year.

The lower G&A rate was primarily due to net revenue leverage, partially offset by investments in our team. Our Q3 operating margin jumped 520 basis points to 11.6% compared to 6.4% last year, while our adjusted EBITDA margin improved 410 basis points to 16.5% compared to 12.4% last year. As a reminder, our Q3 and year-to-date adjusted EBITDA margin only includes the portion of tariff refunds attributable to goods sold in those corresponding periods, excluding any benefits pertaining to fiscal 2025. Net income for the quarter totaled $19.3 million, or diluted EPS of $0.10, compared to net income of $8.7 million last year, or diluted EPS of $0.05.

On our balance sheet, we finished the quarter with record cash equivalents, and short-term investments of $331.2 million, approximately 10% higher than our prior peak in Q4 of 2025. This was a 37% increase year-over-year, even as we allocated approximately $60 million during the nine-month period for share repurchases and shifted to net share settlement of employee equity awards. Inventory decreased 20% year-over-year to $121.3 million, largely given the combined impact of our supplier transitions, strong sales performance, and ongoing optimization efforts. We expect inventory levels to remain down year-over-year for Q4 before beginning to rebuild in 2027.

Share repurchases during the quarter under our ongoing repurchase program totaled approximately $14 million at a weighted average price of $14.02 per share. Inclusive of the previously announced $100 million increase of our authorization, as of quarter end, we had approximately $105 million available for future repurchases under the program. Finally, capital expenditures for the quarter were $5.7 million, reflecting software capitalization, leasehold improvement, and Community Hub-related outlays for our Q4 openings. Now turning to our updated outlook. We have increased our Q4 expectations for net revenues and now expect approximately 15% year-over-year growth for the period. Combined with the underlying strength of our Q3 performance, we now expect full year 2026 net revenue growth of approximately 23.5% year-over-year.

This is ahead of our prior full year outlook, calling for approximately 20% growth and roughly double the high end of our original outlook provided on our February 2026 earnings call. We believe the growth and behavior of our active customer base are powerful indicators of demand, supporting the strong start to the fourth quarter, which included an incredibly successful fall drop in October and early improvements in our core in-stock levels. At the same time, we recognize that a significant share of the quarter remains ahead of us, where we are lapping last year's remarkable strength and execution.

Given the incredible demand we have seen year-to-date, we also believe the growth opportunity could have been higher if we were not experiencing limited edition stock-outs over the near term. Nonetheless, the implied two-year stack for Q4 of nearly 50% is a true testament to the momentum of our business. On the margin side, we now expect our full year GAAP gross margin to be approximately 69.9%, up from our prior outlook of approximately 69.5%.

As disclosed last quarter, this is inclusive of the $15.4 million reduction to cost of goods from the tariff refund recognized during Q2, an additional $1.9 million benefit in Q3 as impacted goods were sold during the period, and our sell-through expectations in Q4 for related inventory. While these refund-related expectations have not changed, the underlying gross margin expectations have improved with strong full price selling, more favorable product mix, and lower air freight than planned. For Q4, we expect a sizable year-over-year improvement, in part due to the large inventory write-off comparison from the year-ago period. Within SG&A, we would note two factors pertaining to Q4 expectations.

First, we expect a higher year-over-year selling expense rate driven by supplier transition costs and ongoing pressure on outbound freight. Second, we expect a lower year-over-year marketing expense rate driven by our ongoing efficiency efforts and the comparison to last year's ramp in Winter Olympics investments. Overall, we have increased our full-year operating margin outlook to approximately 12%, compared to our prior outlook of approximately 10.8%. We have also increased our full-year adjusted EBITDA margin outlook to approximately 15.8%, up from our prior range of between 14.8% and 15%. We are set up for a great finish to a great year. Our full-year net revenue growth is planned to be the highest since 2021.

Our adjusted EBITDA margins are planned to inflect 650 basis points from trough levels in 2024, and our balance sheet has never been stronger. Just as important, we are positioned to sustain both top and bottom line momentum into 2027. We are now happy to take your questions. Operator?

Operator: We will now begin the question and answer session. Please limit yourself to one question and one follow-up. If you would like to ask a question, please press star one to raise your hand. Adrienne Yih: Great. Thank you. Great job.

Sarah Oughtred: Sure. We finished the quarter at $190 million in revenue. It was 26% growth. That was ahead of our expectations. We guided to 20 knowing that this inventory supply would be an issue for us. We did outperform, which was great, but we still feel like we could have done more had we been in a more optimal position. All of that is reflected in the guide for Q4, but we do feel like we are continuing to improve that position each and every day. We think there will be some impact into Q1, but we're working as quickly as possible to improve that inventory supply so that we can keep up with the amazing demand that we're seeing.

As that inventory is sold in Q3 and Q4, we're seeing additional benefit there. We did see an additional $1.9 million in benefit in Q3 and expect to see more in Q4. The benefit to gross margin for all of 2026 is about 250 basis points of benefit.

That will be a headwind on gross margin from a year-over-year perspective, just given that those tariff refunds were one-time in nature. The other component impacting us is also our transition from Jordan into Vietnam. We have a slightly different duty structure, and we are seeing some slightly higher costs just at the onset with some of our fabrications that we can continue to work on over time. Freight has impacted our P&L, both in terms of inbound freight, which would hit our gross margin, as well as outbound freight for our packages going out. For now, we are continuing to monitor.

We're not necessarily planning for any reductions at this point, but if we do see a notable reduction, that could be a benefit into next year. I would say that as we think about gross margin, I think the normalized rate this year would be in that 67.5-ish, and I think we'll kind of be in that range for next year, given some of the puts and takes.

Workiva (WK) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Agent Studio launch and a big bank expansion highlight the quarter.

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

Workiva Inc. (WK +0.26%) reported results for the third quarter of 2026, highlighted by growth in large customer contracts and the launch of new artificial intelligence capabilities. Management stated that the company is transitioning from enabling regulatory work to executing it through agentic solutions. The company reported continued expansion in its large customer cohorts and maintained its full-year guidance for revenue and cash flow while raising its non-GAAP operating margin outlook to a range of 18.2% to 18.3%. Management attributed this performance to steady execution across its solution portfolio and a growing multi-solution footprint, with 77% of subscription revenue now derived from customers using multiple products.

Operator: Good afternoon, ladies and gentlemen. Welcome to Workiva's Q3 2026 Earnings Call. My name is Jamie, and I will be your host operator on this call. After the prepared comments, we will conduct a question-and-answer session. Instructions will be provided at that time. Please note that this call is being recorded on November 5th, 2026, 5:00 P.M. Eastern Time. I would now like to turn the meeting over to your host for today's call, Katie White, Senior Director of Investor Relations at Workiva. Ma'am, please go ahead.

Katie White: Good afternoon, and thank you for joining Workiva's Q3 2026 Conference Call. During today's call, we will review our third quarter results and discuss our guidance for the fourth quarter and full year 2026. Today's call will include comments from our Chief Executive Officer, Julie Iskow, followed by our Chief Financial Officer, Barbara Larson. We will then open up the call for a Q&A session. After market close today, we issued a press release, which is available on our investor relations website, along with our quarterly investor presentation. This conference call is being webcast live, and following the call, an audio replay will be available on our website.

During today's call, we will be making forward-looking statements regarding future events and financial performance, including guidance for the fourth quarter and full fiscal year 2026. These forward-looking statements are based on our assumptions as to the macroeconomic, political, and regulatory environment as of today, reflect management's current expectations and beliefs based on factors currently known to us, and are subject to significant risks and uncertainties. Workiva cautions that these forward-looking statements are not guarantees of future performance. We undertake no obligation to update or revise these statements. If the call is reviewed after today, the information presented during this call may not contain current or accurate information.

Please refer to the company's annual report on Form 10-K and subsequent filings with the SEC for factors that may cause our actual results to differ materially from those contained in our forward-looking statements. Also, during the course of today's call, we will refer to certain non-GAAP financial measures. Reconciliations of GAAP and non-GAAP measures are included in today's press release. With that, we'll begin by turning the call over to Workiva's CEO, Julie Iskow.

Julie Iskow: Thank you, Katie, and thank you all for joining us today. Q3 of 2026 was another solid quarter for Workiva. It was marked by steady execution and consistent demand across our platform. We delivered results above the high end of our guidance range, generating $263 million in total revenue, up 17% year-over-year. This was driven by our 17% growth in subscription revenue. Our ongoing focus on operational discipline continues to be reflected in our expanding margins. Non-GAAP operating margin for our third quarter reached 18.3%. This is an 85 basis point beat on the top end of our guide and a 560 basis point expansion compared to Q3 of last year.

We are also seeing continued growth in our large customer cohorts. In Q3, contracts valued over $300,000 annually grew 31%, and contracts above $500,000 annually grew 20%, both compared to Q3 of 2025. This growth reflects both continued expansion within our existing customer base and the landing of larger multi-solution new logos. Year-to-date, our performance reflects the durability of our business, our expanding value proposition, and the steady momentum across our customer base and our Partner Ecosystem. In September, we welcomed our customers, partners, analysts, and investors to Las Vegas for our annual Amplify conference and 2026 Investor Day.

One message came through clearly: AI is fundamentally changing enterprise software, and it is raising the bar for what customers expect from Workiva. As AI takes on more complex, high-stakes regulatory work, trust becomes even more critical. Enterprises need confidence that AI-driven outputs are governed, traceable, and auditable. That shift plays directly to Workiva's strengths. It creates an opportunity for us to expand our strategic role, moving from enabling critical regulatory work to increasingly taking on more of that work for our customers. As AI becomes more central to the enterprise, our strategic relevance is showing up in our customer wins.

Companies at the forefront of AI, including leading foundation model companies and category creators like SpaceX, are choosing Workiva for critical financial reporting and high-stakes work. Even the companies building the AI economy rely on Workiva when accuracy, control, and public market scrutiny matter most. To build on this momentum and strengthen our platform for Agentic AI, we introduced several strategic advances at Amplify. First, we launched Agent Studio, which enables customers and partners to quickly build, customize, and deploy AI agents directly within Workiva's trusted platform, automating sophisticated business processes without writing code. Second, we launched an Agentic Solution for automated testing that orchestrates evidence, attribute, and testing agents to automate manual workflows with full traceability.

This new GRC offering expands sampling, risk coverage, and testing capacity for our customers without additional manual effort. Third, we launched three new agentic offerings that mark the beginning of Workiva's expansion into a broader universe of regulatory obligations. With these new solutions, we see a growing opportunity to expand our role in managing our customers' increasingly complex regulatory work. This is reinforced by strong early engagement from both customers and partners. This opportunity is already translating into demand, as demonstrated by a Q3 win for our new Agentic Regulatory Solutions. A global American healthcare corporation expanded their 15-year Workiva partnership in a multi-six-figure deal. They added all three of our new Agentic Regulatory Solutions to their existing seven-solution footprint.

This customer is facing complex Bureau of Economic Analysis and country-by-country reporting across more than 200 entities. They are now moving towards standardizing their manual processes on our platform, turning ad hoc compliance into automated end-to-end agentic workflow. The alignment between customer value, platform innovation, and our monetization model is clearly reflected in our deal execution. Our customer wins from Q3 provide meaningful insight into our business, highlighting how organizations are turning to Workiva to solve their most complex interconnected reporting and compliance challenges. I'd like to highlight a few Q3 deals that demonstrate our continued success as a multi-solution platform company.

First, one of the world's largest custody banks nearly doubled its spend with Workiva through a seven-figure deal that was focused on expansion across the office of the CFO. This 15-year Workiva customer expanded its platform footprint across banking, Multi-Entity Reporting, investments, and Controls Management. As part of a broader finance transformation, the firm is bringing internal management, and external regulatory reporting onto Workiva. It is replacing fragmented processes with a single connected reporting environment. This deal was a co-sell and will be delivered by a Big Four firm. Second, we signed a high six-figure new logo deal with a large U.S. consumer products firm preparing for its next chapter as an independent public company.

This customer purchased six solutions: SEC Reporting, Sustainability Reporting, Controls Management, Tax Reporting, Enterprise Risk Management, and Audit Management. The purchase was driven by the need to establish a trusted connected platform from day one, unifying reporting, controls, and risk in a single governed environment. This deal was a co-sell and will be delivered by a Big Four firm. Third, we signed a mid-six-figure new logo deal with one of the world's largest mining companies. The customer purchased five solutions spanning SEC reporting, Controls Management, Sustainability Reporting, Management Reporting, and financial statements. The deal was driven by a broader transformation initiative to automate reporting and consolidate fragmented workflows onto a single integrated platform.

This competitive win over legacy financial printers and financial reporting software providers highlights the differentiation of Workiva's connected multi-solution platform. The deal was a co-sell and will be delivered by a Big Four firm. I will turn now to financial reporting, where the demands on finance teams continue to evolve. As customers leverage AI to take on more of the reporting process, the need for trusted data, governance, and traceability only increases. This shift plays directly to Workiva's strengths in delivering accurate, governed, and fully traceable financial reporting. Here are a few notable Q3 deals that illustrate this momentum. First, a global consumer goods company signed a high six-figure deal for Multi-Entity Reporting and Sustainability Reporting.

The company is executing a multi-year finance transformation that includes migrating to SAP's/4HANA, standardizing processes across global markets, increasing automation and AI adoption, and improving its ability to respond to evolving compliance requirements. As part of this transformation, this company is bringing more than 200 entities onto Workiva's Multi-Entity Reporting solution, while at the same time expanding its use of Sustainability Reporting across the organization. The deal was sourced and will be delivered by a regional advisory firm. Second, a U.K.-based packaging company landed as a new customer with a mid-six-figure deal spanning three solutions: ESEF Reporting, SEC reporting, and Sustainability Reporting.

With reporting obligations across both Europe and the U.S., the company selected Workiva to consolidate its reporting processes on a single connected platform to improve reporting visibility and to support requirements across multiple regulatory frameworks. This was a competitive win over a financial printer. The deal was sourced and will be delivered by a Big Four firm. Third, a leading global asset manager signed a mid-six-figure expansion deal. This 11-year customer added Multi-Entity Reporting and investment reporting while expanding its use of Controls Management. The expansion is part of a broader finance transformation that is focused on replacing fragmented manual reporting processes with a single connected platform.

Through a direct integration with the company's ERP, the customer is establishing a trusted governed data hub on Workiva that supports reporting across the organization. This deal was a co-sell and will be delivered by a Big Four firm. Let's move on now to financial services. Banks, insurers, and asset managers operate in highly complex regulatory environments. From Pillar 3 filings to reporting across thousands of investment funds, institutions are moving beyond legacy systems and manual processes. They are turning to Workiva to automate and modernize how regulatory reporting gets done. Here are a few deals from the quarter in this vertical.

First, a top 20 global bank signed a high six-figure expansion deal as part of a finance transformation project. The customer added our advanced SEC reporting and ESEF reporting solutions and expanded its use of Workiva to support Basel Pillar 3 regulatory reporting. This was a competitive win over a legacy on-premise financial printer solution. The result is further consolidation of critical financial and regulatory reporting workflows on the Workiva platform. The deal was sourced and will be delivered by a Big Four partner. Second, a privately held diversified financial services company signed a mid-six-figure expansion deal. This existing customer expanded into investment reporting and insurance reporting. The expansion was driven by a company transformation project.

The goal is to standardize reporting on a single platform alongside a broader ERP migration. By automating more of the regulatory processes, the customer is leaning on Workiva to improve efficiency and create a consolidated view across the enterprise. The deal was a co-sell and will be delivered by a Big Four firm. Turning now to Governance, Risk, and Compliance. Demand across our GRC portfolio remains strong as enterprises accelerate vendor consolidation. Chief Audit Executives are actively replacing incumbent single-purpose GRC tools with Workiva's unified platform. By managing Audit workflows, controls, and risk management in the same environment as core corporate reporting, our customers achieve continuous audit-ready assurance while driving out the cost of legacy point solutions.

Here are a couple of our Q3 GRC wins. First, a Fortune 100 technology company signed a high six-figure expansion deal for Audit Management, compliance management, Controls Management, and Policies and Procedures. This longtime financial reporting customer is replacing a manual supplier risk assessment process with Workiva's connected GRC platform. This was a competitive win over a GRC point solution. The deal was sourced and will be delivered by a Big Four firm. Second, a global agricultural solutions business signed a mid-six-figure expansion deal for eight solutions: Audit Management, Compliance Management, Controls Management, Operational Risk Management, Policies and Procedures, Global Statutory Reporting, Carbon, and Sustainability.

The primary driver was replacing manual Excel-based workflows with a single integrated reporting process across its global entities and sites, all on the Workiva connected platform. Following a competitive RFP, Workiva was selected over a European consolidation and reporting software provider. Next, I'll turn to sustainability. Workiva is well-positioned in sustainability because these disclosures are increasingly demanding the same rigor as financial reporting, trusted data, strong controls, clear lineage, and assurance readiness. As sustainability becomes more integrated with regulatory reporting, responsibility is moving closer to the office of the CFO. As organizations take a pragmatic multi-year approach to meeting these evolving expectations, the value of Workiva's unified platform is clear.

Workiva brings financial and Sustainability Reporting together on one governed platform, giving organizations the control and the transparency to manage complex multi-entity requirements as they evolve. The institution has a well-established sustainability and emissions reporting program with greenhouse gas disclosures dating back to 2006. As these reporting demands expand, the customer has doubled its spend with Workiva, extending Sustainability Reporting across multiple entities on our platform. The deal was a co-sell and will be implemented by a regional advisory firm. Finally, let us close with Capital Markets, where we were pleased to support several IPOs in Q3, including Lime, Braveheart, and Orion180.

In recent weeks, we supported one of the most high-profile S-1 filings to date, as one of the world's leading frontier AI companies prepares for their public debut. For Workiva, the opportunity extends well beyond IPO. We often engage with high-growth private companies one or two years before they go public, establishing the trusted data foundation and the audit-ready controls required for public company reporting. By the time a customer files its S-1, Workiva is already embedded in their critical workflows, creating a natural expansion path into SEC Reporting, Controls Management, Multi-Entity Reporting, and other solutions as they scale. Beyond IPOs, our capital market solutions also support customers through complex M&A transactions.

In Q3, a 14-year platform customer expanded its investment in Workiva with a high six-figure deal to support a transformational business combination and the preparation and the assembly of their S-4 disclosures. For complex M&A, Workiva brings critical transaction workstreams into a single audit-ready environment, helping customers prepare transaction filings, manage SEC review cycles, and accelerate deal execution. This win demonstrates how high-stakes corporate events can create significant expansion opportunities across our customer base. Looking across financial reporting, GRC, sustainability, and our recently launched regulatory solutions, we remain confident in the long-term opportunity across our portfolio. Enterprise software is evolving from supporting and automating processes to actively executing more of the work.

That shift plays directly to Workiva's strengths: trusted data, deeply embedded workflows, enterprise-grade governance, and a platform built for the complex high-stakes work of the office of the CFO. We believe this evolution expands the value we can deliver to customers, and importantly, the opportunity within our existing customer base. As our platform takes on more of these critical processes, we have more ways to deepen customer relationships, expand adoption, and drive durable growth at scale. Barbara Larson: Thanks, Julie. I will begin by walking through our third quarter financial results and key metrics, then share our guidance for the fourth quarter and full year 2026. We saw steady execution across the business in Q3, with broad-based demand across our solution portfolio. Total revenue for the quarter was $263 million, up 17% year-over-year and exceeding the high end of our guidance by $1 million. Moving down the P&L to net income and earnings per share. Beginning in Q3, we are applying an effective tax rate of 22% to our non-GAAP results, reflecting our sustained profitability. In prior quarters, our non-GAAP results incorporated our GAAP tax expense, which was relatively nominal. On this new basis, non-GAAP net income was $41 million, up 55% year-over-year, and non-GAAP net income per diluted share was $0.72, up from $0.46 in Q3 last year.

Our prior guidance did not assume this 22% tax rate. To help with your models, our press release and investor presentation recast prior quarters at the new rate. This change affects non-GAAP presentation only. It has no impact on actual cash taxes, cash flow, or our GAAP results. On a GAAP basis, net income for the quarter was $19 million, up from $3 million in Q3 last year. GAAP net income per diluted share was $0.35, up from $0.05 a year ago and $0.10 above the high end of our guidance range. Moving on to our performance metrics for the quarter. As of the end of the third quarter, we had 2,853 contracts valued at over $100,000 per year, up 20% year over year. Contracts valued at over $300,000 totaled 708, up 31% over the prior year.

Contracts valued at over $500,000 reached 283, up 20% from Q3 2025. Next, let's review our balance sheet and cash flows. As of quarter end, we have repurchased a total of $246 million under our $350 million share repurchase program, with $104 million remaining as we entered the fourth quarter. Moving on to guidance. Our guidance assumes a slight year-over-year FX headwind to projected revenue growth in Q4. With that in mind, for the fourth quarter of 2026, we expect total revenue to range from $275 million-$278 million. We expect services revenue to be up slightly compared to Q4 2025, and we expect non-GAAP operating margin to be in the range of 19%-19.5%. For the full year 2026, we expect total revenue to range from $1.041 billion-$1.044 billion.

We continue to expect subscription revenue to grow approximately 19% year over year, and we expect total services revenue to be up moderately year-over-year. We are raising our non-GAAP operating margin outlook by 25 basis points at the midpoint compared to our prior guidance, and now expect it to be in the range of 18.2%-18.3%. At the midpoint, this represents an 835 basis point improvement year-over-year, reflecting our continued focus on driving operating leverage as we scale. Finally, we continue to expect our free cash flow margin to be approximately 21% for the year.
